# Subject Benchmark Statement: Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying (QAA, April 2024)

> Run 0113, P1 by std. The issuer's words are held in the source library, `wkspc2601-library/txt/qaa-2024-land-construction-real-estate-surveying-subject-standard.norm.txt`; each S4 record carries its statement in full (`source`) for language analysis, with its span. Paraphrases and versions are ours. Private to the project, never published. In front matter `status` is the issuer's; ours is `lifecycle`. Doubts are in Part E.

## Part 0 Summary

### 0.1 Agent definition
<!-- label: editorial; status: drafted; basis: primary -->
QAA's UK reference point for what a graduate in land, construction, real estate or surveying should know and be able to do by the end of an honours degree, with a short statement for master's study. Reach for it when designing, reviewing or describing a course in building surveying, quantity surveying, construction management, building control and fire engineering, real estate, rural land, planning and development or geospatial surveying. It sets threshold standards only and leaves the classes above them to Annex D; it is not a curriculum, not a professional body's accreditation standard and not a regulatory requirement in England.

### 0.2 Summary
<!-- label: both; status: drafted; basis: primary -->
Published by QAA in April 2024 as the fifth edition, the statement covers the built environment subjects of land, construction, real estate and surveying in eight indicative categories. It sets out the subject's context and purposes, five cross-cutting themes as the issuer names them (equality, diversity and inclusion; accessibility; sustainable development; employability, enterprise and entrepreneurship; information management), with generative AI in teaching, then distinctive features including professional accreditation and degree apprenticeships, and content, teaching and assessment. Its benchmark standards are threshold only: seven groups of generic skills and nine subject-specific lists for the honours degree, four master's outcomes and a pointer to the FHEQ level 7 descriptor; achievement above threshold is referred to Annex D. It names RICS, CIOB, CICES and CABE among the accrediting bodies, and the Building Safety Regulator, Ofsted and IfATE.

  about: "applying EDI and accessibility in professional practice"
  themes: [theme:edi]
  domain: generic-skill
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Graduates are expected to bring professional standards and knowledge to bear in embedding EDI and accessibility at work, answerable to industry and society; they should take EDI into account across a project's life cycle and its effects on diverse end users."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: both
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.14
  locator: { number: "1.14", headingPath: ["Context and purposes of a Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degree", "Sustainability"] }
  span: { start: 16295, end: 17068 }
  source: "1.14 Sustainability is about how development avoids exacerbating contemporary issues - such as climate change and environmental degradation, while promoting positive processes such as the circular economy, the move to net zero and good environmental and social governance. Graduates should respond to these by behaving ethically, protecting and enhancing the quality of life, sustaining communities, prosperity, health and planetary well- being. The challenges raised by sustainable development are met by the actions of graduates balancing the environmental, economic as well as social needs and justices of current generations with those of the future. As such, graduates are expected to uphold these environmental, economic and social values as qualified practitioners.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: fheq:6
  appliesTo: "graduates of the subject"
  addressee: learner
  verbs: ["respond", "behaving", "protecting", "enhancing", "sustaining", "uphold"]
  about: "responding ethically to sustainability challenges"
  themes: [theme:esd]
  domain: generic-skill
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Sustainability means development that does not worsen problems such as climate change while advancing the circular economy, net zero and good governance; graduates should meet this by acting ethically for people, communities and the planet, balancing present and future needs; they are expected to hold to environmental, economic and social values in practice."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: both
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.15
  locator: { number: "1.15", headingPath: ["Context and purposes of a Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degree", "Sustainability"] }
  span: { start: 17068, end: 17531 }
  source: "1.15 In the search for intergenerational equity, graduates can explore how they can contribute towards the UN Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs)\n\nand HABITAT Agenda, along with the targets these international agencies set for development to be sustainable. Here consideration should also be given to the UK statement on the SDGs, and those\n\n6\nagendas relating to England, Wales, Scotland and Northern Ireland, including key stakeholders such as RICS and CIOB .\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "courses and graduates of the subject"
  addressee: both
  verbs: ["explore", "contribute", "given"]
  about: "the SDGs, the HABITAT Agenda and national agendas"
  themes: [theme:esd]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Graduates can explore how they might contribute to the UN Sustainable Development Goals and the HABITAT Agenda and their targets; the UK's statement on the SDGs and the agendas of the four nations, with stakeholders such as RICS and CIOB, should also be considered."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: both
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.16
  locator: { number: "1.16", headingPath: ["Context and purposes of a Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degree", "Sustainability"] }
  span: { start: 17531, end: 18409 }
  source: "1.16 Higher education providers should ensure a balanced and innovative pedagogical approach on the theory and practice of sustainability in the course curricula that incorporates assessment. Taught provision also aligns with the learning outcomes found in the Education for Sustainable Development Guidance produced by Advance HE and QAA (March 2021). Discipline-specific and transferable skills shall be evenly cultivated, recognising that interdisciplinary knowledge and understanding are key to growth as recent studies of sustainable development have highlighted. The curricula for all the courses listed in this Statement can adopt the UNESCO key competencies for sustainable development: systems thinking, anticipatory studies, normative analysis and interpretation, strategic alliances, collaboration, critical reflection, self-awareness and integrated problem-solving.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "higher education providers"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["ensure", "incorporates", "aligns", "cultivated", "adopt"]
  about: "sustainability pedagogy and its assessment"
  themes: [theme:esd]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Providers should make sure the curriculum takes a balanced, innovative approach to the theory and practice of sustainability, including in assessment; provision aligns with the outcomes of the Advance HE and QAA ESD Guidance, subject and transferable skills are to be developed evenly ('shall'), and courses can adopt the UNESCO key competencies."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.18
  locator: { number: "1.18", headingPath: ["Context and purposes of a Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degree", "Sustainability"] }
  span: { start: 18941, end: 19319 }
  source: "1.18 Such opportunities should be designed to inculcate and embed ethical behaviour and environmental, social and governance (ESG) values aligned to the requirements of accrediting professional bodies and industry best practice. A key driver of sector employers is to enhance students' awareness of corporate responsibility. Employability, enterprise and entrepreneurship (EEE)\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "sustainability learning opportunities"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["designed", "inculcate", "embed", "enhance"]
  about: "embedding ethics and ESG values"
  themes: [theme:esd]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Sustainability learning should be designed to instil ethical conduct and ESG values in line with accrediting bodies and industry good practice; employers want students more aware of corporate responsibility."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.21
  locator: { number: "1.21", headingPath: ["Context and purposes of a Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degree", "Employability, enterprise and entrepreneurship (EEE)"] }
  span: { start: 20066, end: 20648 }
  source: "1.21 Courses will prepare students for evolving business environments, including rapid technological change. This includes embedding commercial and corporate awareness through providing impactful learning experiences in both real-world and simulated placements and activities that build links between academic institutions and external organisations. Beyond employment, entrepreneurship education provides competencies to help students lead a rewarding, self-determined, professional life; well placed to add social, cultural and economic value to society throughout their careers.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: will
  level: none
  appliesTo: "courses in the subject"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["prepare", "embedding", "providing"]
  about: "preparing students for changing business environments"
  themes: [theme:enterprise]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Courses will ready students for changing business settings and fast technological change, building commercial and corporate awareness through real and simulated placements and activities that link universities and outside organisations; entrepreneurship education helps students lead a self-directed professional life that adds value to society."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.26
  locator: { number: "1.26", headingPath: ["Context and purposes of a Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degree", "Information management"] }
  span: { start: 23152, end: 23418 }
  source: "1.26 Information is key in the Industry 4.0 / 5.0 revolutions of the future. LCRES courses will use digital tools such as building information modelling (BIM) and artificial intelligence (AI) that enable the capture, manipulation, storage and dissemination of data.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: will
  level: none
  appliesTo: "courses in the subject"
  addressee: both
  verbs: ["use", "enable"]
  about: "digital tools such as BIM and AI"
  themes: [theme:information-management]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Information drives the coming Industry 4.0 and 5.0 changes, so courses will use digital tools such as BIM and AI for capturing, handling, storing and sharing data."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: both
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.28.b1
  locator: { number: "1.28.b1", headingPath: ["Context and purposes of a Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degree", "Information management"] }
  span: { start: 24425, end: 25377 }
  source: "Information management structures and systems. In this area graduates should demonstrate competence in the use of electronic information handling and data processing using appropriate analysis software and applications using digital information systems. They should be competent in the use and interpretation of digital data and information to inform decision-making. This will involve handing electronic information with guidance, and application of appropriate standards (such as ISO19650), techniques, digital tools (BIM, for example) and applications. They should understand general information management practices to digitally organise and manage information and records. An understanding and appreciation of the need for\n\n8\ndata protection, security and privacy (GDPR, for example) to help maintain the accessibility, security, confidentiality, integrity, availability and accountability of information systems are key competencies of graduates."
  stem: "1.28"
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: fheq:6
  appliesTo: "graduates of the subject"
  addressee: learner
  verbs: ["demonstrate", "understand"]
  about: "information management structures and systems"
  themes: [theme:information-management]
  domain: generic-skill
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "In information management structures and systems, graduates should show competence in handling electronic information and processing data with suitable software and digital systems, use and interpret digital data for decisions, apply standards such as ISO 19650 and tools such as BIM, understand how to organise and manage information and records, and appreciate data protection, security and privacy."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: both
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.28.b2
  locator: { number: "1.28.b2", headingPath: ["Context and purposes of a Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degree", "Information management"] }
  span: { start: 25380, end: 25943 }
  source: "Use of digital technologies. In this area graduates will have the knowledge and be able to apply the use of relevant digital technologies (BIM, for example) in their field. They will employ the application of information technology and digital tools and techniques to support key subjects. Graduates should have an understanding of how AI can promote efficiency in the project life cycle by decreasing errors and undertaking repetitive tasks. Further advancements in analysing and optimising the design, construction and use of facilities is a must for graduates."
  stem: "1.28"
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: will
  level: fheq:6
  appliesTo: "graduates of the subject"
  addressee: learner
  verbs: ["apply", "employ", "support"]
  about: "use of digital technologies, including AI"
  themes: [theme:information-management]
  domain: generic-skill
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "In the use of digital technologies, graduates will know and apply relevant tools such as BIM in their field and use IT and digital techniques in key subjects; they should understand how AI can make the project life cycle more efficient, and pursuing better analysis and optimisation of design, construction and use is called a must."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: both
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.28.b3
  locator: { number: "1.28.b3", headingPath: ["Context and purposes of a Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degree", "Information management"] }
  span: { start: 25946, end: 26557 }
  source: "Digital literacies. In this area graduates should recognise the use of online platforms, being aware of the limitations as a means of communication and a source of information. Graduates need to use a range of information and communication technology (ICT) platforms (for example, desktop, server, tablet and mobile) and social media to communicate information to a range of audiences effectively. Graduates should demonstrate an awareness of the legal, effective and safe use of digital technologies and social media, alongside a heightened awareness of the safe, ethical and legal use of digital technologies."
  stem: "1.28"
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: fheq:6
  appliesTo: "graduates of the subject"
  addressee: learner
  verbs: ["recognise", "use", "demonstrate"]
  about: "digital literacies: platforms, ICT and safe use"
  themes: [theme:information-management]
  domain: generic-skill
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "In digital literacies, graduates should recognise online platforms and their limits for communication and information, use a range of ICT platforms and social media to reach different audiences, and show awareness of legal, safe and ethical use of digital technology and social media."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: both
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/2.4
  locator: { number: "2.4", headingPath: ["Distinctive features of Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degrees", "Design of courses"] }
  span: { start: 28501, end: 29203 }
  source: "2.4 LCRES courses are concerned with linking knowledge, skills and understanding with application in practice which could be seen as enabling competence as well as capability. Courses should be designed to enable students to develop a wide range of communication skills, critical thinking, problem-solving, self-management, data and information management. The ability to collaborate and work in teams, achieve practical solutions and respect colleagues are fundamental attributes. This focus is illustrated by the example of interdisciplinary learning around sustainability. These transferable skills are developed progressively and in parallel with the specific subject-related competencies studied.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "courses in the subject"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["designed", "enable", "develop"]
  about: "designing courses for transferable skills"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Courses link knowledge, skills and understanding to practice; they should be designed so students build communication, critical thinking, problem-solving, self-management and data and information skills, with teamwork and respect for colleagues as core attributes developed progressively beside subject competencies."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/2.7
  locator: { number: "2.7", headingPath: ["Distinctive features of Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degrees", "Accessibility"] }
  span: { start: 30826, end: 31101 }
  source: "2.7 All higher education providers have an obligation to ensure accessibility for those wishing to study. This is no different in LCRES courses, and the expectations set out previously around EDI (see paragraphs 1.8-1.13) apply equally to all courses aligned with the topic.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: requirement
  rung: none
  force: must   # D-std-0016: "obligation" read as must
  level: none
  appliesTo: "all higher education providers"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["ensure", "apply"]
  about: "providers' obligation to ensure accessibility"
  themes: [theme:accessibility]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "All providers are obliged to make study accessible to those who wish to study, and the EDI expectations of 1.8 to 1.13 apply to every course in the subject."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/2.9
  locator: { number: "2.9", headingPath: ["Distinctive features of Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degrees", "Accessibility"] }
  span: { start: 31304, end: 31999 }
  source: "2.9 The varied nature of key modules in LCRES courses requires students to engage with a number of different learning and assessment types. These should recognise and respect the diverse perspectives and learning styles found among students by clearly expressing the learning outcomes and how these will be assessed. Course teams should devise assessments that can be adjusted to meet the needs or circumstances of an individual student in a manner that is in keeping with institutional guidelines. A student benefitting from any reasonable adjustment to an assessment should also have access to appropriate support services in line with national frameworks and institutional service provision.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "course teams"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["recognise", "respect", "devise", "adjusted"]
  about: "diverse assessment and reasonable adjustments"
  themes: [theme:accessibility]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Varied learning and assessment types should respect students' diverse perspectives and styles by stating outcomes and how they will be assessed clearly; course teams should design assessments that can be adjusted for individual needs within institutional rules, and students with adjustments should be able to reach support services."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/2.14
  locator: { number: "2.14", headingPath: ["Distinctive features of Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degrees", "Flexibility"] }
  span: { start: 34377, end: 34925 }
  source: "2.14 There are numerous different examples of industry engagement within higher education providers that undoubtedly enhance the quality of teaching delivered, especially in the areas of promoting future employability. Industry input may also take the form of guest lectures, 'live' projects, context materials such as case studies and scenarios as well as the opportunity to provide students with work placements. Within LCRES courses, there is an expectation that students will undertake a work-related project and/or a dissertation. Partnership\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: will
  level: fheq:6
  appliesTo: "students of the subject"
  addressee: both
  verbs: ["undertake"]
  about: "a work-related project or dissertation"
  themes: [none]
  domain: subject-skill
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Industry engagement, through guest lectures, live projects, case material and placements, improves teaching and employability, and students are expected to complete a work-related project, a dissertation or both."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: both
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/2.22
  locator: { number: "2.22", headingPath: ["Distinctive features of Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degrees", "Apprenticeships"] }
  span: { start: 39368, end: 39873 }
  source: "2.22 In developing and delivering a successful degree apprenticeship providers across the UK must engage with employers and apprentices to plan and monitor their training over the duration of the apprenticeship. This includes consideration of 'on and off the job' training and how these interplay with the degree apprenticeship award. Trainers, coaches and mentors must communicate up-to-date vocational and technical subject knowledge that reflects expected industry practice and meets employers' needs.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: requirement
  rung: none
  force: must
  level: none
  appliesTo: "providers of degree apprenticeships across the UK"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["engage", "plan", "monitor", "communicate"]
  about: "apprenticeship delivery with employers"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["degree apprenticeship"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "To deliver a degree apprenticeship well, providers across the UK must work with employers and apprentices to plan and monitor training throughout, including how on- and off-the-job training fit the award; trainers, coaches and mentors must pass on current vocational and technical knowledge that matches industry practice and employers' needs."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/2.23
  locator: { number: "2.23", headingPath: ["Distinctive features of Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degrees", "Apprenticeships"] }
  span: { start: 39873, end: 40385 }
  source: "2.23 In England, , the award of an integrated degree apprenticeship must fully meet the requirements of the occupational standard and its end-point assessment (EPA) plan for full achievement of the degree and apprenticeship award. At the end of the integrated degree apprenticeship, providers of the course must follow the full requirements of the EPA plan to ensure independent assessment of occupational competence, as defined by the relevant occupational standard’s end-point assessment plan, is carried out.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: requirement
  rung: none
  force: must
  level: none
  appliesTo: "providers of integrated degree apprenticeships in England"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["meet", "follow", "ensure"]
  about: "integrated apprenticeships and the end-point assessment"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["degree apprenticeship"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "In England an integrated degree apprenticeship must fully meet its occupational standard and end-point assessment plan for the degree and apprenticeship to be achieved, and at its end providers must follow the plan in full so that occupational competence is independently assessed."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/2.24
  locator: { number: "2.24", headingPath: ["Distinctive features of Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degrees", "Apprenticeships"] }
  span: { start: 40385, end: 40733 }
  source: "2.24 Providers in England will need to identify and train appropriately qualified and competent assessors to carry out independent EPAs; these may be industry professionals and practitioners. Only when the apprentice successfully completes the independent EPA element of an integrated degree apprenticeship can their degree certificate be awarded.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: requirement
  rung: none
  force: must
  level: none
  appliesTo: "providers of degree apprenticeships in England"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["identify", "train", "completes", "awarded"]
  about: "assessors for independent end-point assessment"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["degree apprenticeship"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Providers in England will need to find and train qualified, competent assessors, who may come from industry, for independent EPAs; an integrated degree apprenticeship's degree certificate can be awarded only once the apprentice passes the independent EPA."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/2.25
  locator: { number: "2.25", headingPath: ["Distinctive features of Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying degrees", "Apprenticeships"] }
  span: { start: 40733, end: 41147 }
  source: "2.25 In the case of a non-integrated degree apprenticeship, providers in England must deliver the degree stated in the specific occupational standard and be aware that full completion of the degree award may be required as a prerequisite to EPA gateway. External EPA organisations are responsible for the independent assessment through final assessment of competence and full completion of the apprenticeship.\n\n13\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: requirement
  rung: none
  force: must
  level: none
  appliesTo: "providers of non-integrated degree apprenticeships in England"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["deliver", "be"]
  about: "non-integrated degree apprenticeships"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["degree apprenticeship"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "For a non-integrated degree apprenticeship in England, providers must deliver the degree the occupational standard names and know that completing it may be required before the EPA gateway; external EPA organisations carry out the independent final assessment."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited

- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.9
  locator: { number: "3.9", headingPath: ["Content, structure and delivery", "Learning, teaching and assessment practices"] }
  span: { start: 46522, end: 47542 }
  source: "3.9 The diversity of Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying courses has led to a rich variety of teaching, learning and assessment practices. All LCRES subject categories span theoretical, practical and professional activities which influence the subject-specific pedagogical approach. Fundamentally, learning and teaching practices comprise a pedagogy that embraces the practical application of theory and will support the development of professional competencies. A constructive alignment model is favoured, in which both teaching methods and assessment activities are aligned with module learning outcomes. This will be reflected in assessment practices that both measure the learning outcomes of the programme and support student's learning. Learning, teaching and assessment practices should facilitate conceptual understanding, the application of knowledge and reflection on performance. Learning and teaching practices should develop student resilience and promote lifelong learning. Learning and teaching\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "learning, teaching and assessment practice"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["facilitate", "develop", "promote"]
  about: "aims of learning, teaching and assessment practice"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Teaching applies theory in practice and builds professional competence through constructive alignment of teaching and assessment with outcomes; practice should foster conceptual understanding, application and reflection on performance, and should build resilience and lifelong learning."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.10
  locator: { number: "3.10", headingPath: ["Content, structure and delivery", "Learning and teaching"] }
  span: { start: 47542, end: 48109 }
  source: "3.10 Learning and teaching practices should be tailored to different learning environments, whether situated within the lecture theatre, a tutorial, online or in the workplace. The delivery method, including blended approaches, should be appropriate to the subject matter. Learning and teaching practices, where appropriate, should embed the following distinctive features of LCRES courses set out in sections 1 and 2 of this Statement:\n• inclusive practices\n\n• sustainable development\n• employability\n• information management skills that are integral to the course.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "learning and teaching practice"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["tailored", "embed"]
  about: "fitting delivery to setting and embedding the themes"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Teaching should suit its setting, from lecture theatre to workplace, delivery should fit the material; where suitable, practice should embed inclusive practice, sustainable development, employability and information management skills."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.11
  locator: { number: "3.11", headingPath: ["Content, structure and delivery", "Learning and teaching"] }
  span: { start: 48109, end: 48753 }
  source: "3.11 Learning and teaching practices should develop the application of knowledge and skills stated in the programme learning outcomes and, in addition, enable students to make critical connections across modules and contextualise their learning within the LCRES sector. The ultimate goal of student learning is the considered application of knowledge and skills together with an appreciation of the integrative nature of the subject areas in an appropriate context. As such, teaching approaches that incorporate live projects, case studies, project simulations, role play, specialist external lectures and collaborative working are encouraged.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "learning and teaching practice"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["develop", "enable", "make", "contextualise"]
  about: "applying learning and connecting across modules"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Teaching should develop the application of the programme's stated knowledge and skills and help students connect modules critically and place their learning in the sector; live projects, cases, simulations, role play, external lectures and collaboration are encouraged."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.12
  locator: { number: "3.12", headingPath: ["Content, structure and delivery", "Learning and teaching"] }
  span: { start: 48753, end: 49097 }
  source: "3.12 Learning and teaching practices should consider student cohorts' differing starting points and associated student journeys (as reflected in their chosen mode of study) and reflect this in the pedagogic approach. The use of practice-based scenarios is encouraged to support students whenever there is no opportunity of work-based learning.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "learning and teaching practice"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["consider", "reflect"]
  about: "differing starting points and modes of study"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Teaching should take account of cohorts' different starting points and routes through study, as seen in their mode of study, and reflect this in pedagogy; practice-based scenarios are encouraged where work-based learning is not possible."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.13
  locator: { number: "3.13", headingPath: ["Content, structure and delivery", "Learning and teaching"] }
  span: { start: 49097, end: 49300 }
  source: "3.13 Within the physical and digital learning environments, learning and teaching practices should recognise different learning styles and preferences and therefore offer a range of teaching approaches.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "learning and teaching practice"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["recognise", "offer"]
  about: "varied teaching for different learning preferences"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "In physical and digital settings alike, teaching should allow for different learning styles and preferences and so use a range of approaches."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.14
  locator: { number: "3.14", headingPath: ["Content, structure and delivery", "Learning and teaching"] }
  span: { start: 49300, end: 49648 }
  source: "3.14 As well as practice-informed teaching, students learning experiences should be supported by research-informed teaching. This can be a combination of research-led, research-oriented, research-tutored or research-based learning. The latter may include students undertaking independent research in the form of research projects or dissertations.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "learning and teaching practice"
  addressee: provider

  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Alongside practice-informed teaching, learning should be backed by research-informed teaching, led, oriented, tutored or based on research, which may include students' own research projects or dissertations."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)

  span: { start: 49648, end: 49741 }
  source: "3.15 Learning and teaching practices should promote academic and professional integrity.\n\n16\n"

- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.16
  locator: { number: "3.16", headingPath: ["Content, structure and delivery", "Learning and teaching"] }
  span: { start: 49741, end: 50213 }
  source: "3.16 Learning and Teaching practices should consider the appropriate use of generative Artificial Intelligence (AI) in authentic learning to reflect the evolving use of AI in the workplace. When using AI to support learning, consideration should be given to developing deep learning and critical thinking skills. Practices should also consider the ethical use of AI, such as robustness of data, sustainability, integrity, inclusivity and wider societal issues. Assessment\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "learning and teaching practice"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["consider", "reflect", "given"]
  about: "generative AI in learning and its ethics"
  themes: [theme:generative-ai]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Teaching should consider proper use of generative AI in authentic learning, reflecting its growing use at work; deep learning and critical thinking should be developed where AI supports learning, and the ethics of AI, including data robustness, sustainability, integrity and inclusion, should be considered."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.17
  locator: { number: "3.17", headingPath: ["Content, structure and delivery", "Assessment"] }
  span: { start: 50213, end: 50818 }
  source: "3.17 Assessment and feedback strategies support learning and enable students to demonstrate their level of attainment and identify their progress. For each method of assessment, the aims, content requirements and marking criteria should be clearly defined. They can be aligned to reflect the diversity of knowledge and skills developed within the curriculum, the approaches to teaching and learning used, and the learning outcomes of the course. Assessment should be accessible to students and, as discussed in paragraph 1.12, alternative assessments should be provided whenever and wherever appropriate.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "assessment practice"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["defined", "provided"]
  about: "clear criteria and accessible assessment"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Assessment and feedback support learning and show attainment and progress; for every method the purpose, required content and marking criteria should be clearly set and assessment should be accessible, with alternatives offered where appropriate."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.18
  locator: { number: "3.18", headingPath: ["Content, structure and delivery", "Assessment"] }
  span: { start: 50818, end: 51571 }
  source: "3.18 Assessment practices should adopt the guiding principles outlined in the UK Quality Code for Higher Education, Advice and Guidance, Assessment. A wide range of assessment methods should be adopted to effectively measure the extent to which students have successfully achieved the intended learning outcomes. Summative and formative assessments should also embrace assessment 'for, as and of learning' as set out in Advance HE and QAA's Education for Sustainable Development Guidance (Figure 1). Incorporating opportunities for self-assessment, peer assessment and practitioner assessment in order to promote self-learning, observation, teamwork skills and commercial awareness are also encouraged.\n\nFigure 1: Assessment for, as and of Learning\n\n17\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "assessment practice"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["adopt", "embrace"]
  about: "assessment principles and the range of methods"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Assessment should follow the Quality Code's Advice and Guidance on Assessment, use a wide range of methods to measure how far outcomes are met, and embrace assessment for, as and of learning as in the ESD Guidance; self, peer and practitioner assessment are encouraged."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.19
  locator: { number: "3.19", headingPath: ["Content, structure and delivery", "Assessment"] }
  span: { start: 51571, end: 52225 }
  source: "3.19 Assessment design should be such that a student can identify its relevance and value beyond their higher education studies. Authentic assessments provide a meaningful context in which students can use and apply their knowledge and skills. As such, the use of scenarios with tasks linked to the learning outcomes of the module are encouraged. Examples include using professional and work-based contexts and adopting specific professional roles, requiring students to demonstrate professional competence. Authentic assessments should also provide the opportunity to localise the tasks to support both the diversity of the sector and its global reach.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "assessment practice"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["identify", "provide"]
  about: "authentic assessment"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["none"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Assessment should be designed so students can see its value beyond their studies; authentic tasks give meaningful contexts, scenarios linked to module outcomes are encouraged, and authentic assessment should allow tasks to be localised for the sector's diversity and global reach."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.21
  locator: { number: "3.21", headingPath: ["Content, structure and delivery", "Independent assessment of degree apprenticeships"] }
  span: { start: 53402, end: 54357 }
  source: "3.21 The great strength of independent assessment is a judgement of an apprentice's occupational competence made by assessors with no vested interest in the outcome of the assessment and who are experts in, and respected by, the occupation. Professional bodies acting as the independent support services provider will ensure that competency-based assessment is independent. This mitigates potential conflicts of interest relating to all assessments of an apprentice. The independent assessor must not have been involved in the training or employment of the apprentice. Specifically, in England, they must not receive a personal benefit or detriment from the result of the assessment and must only deem an apprentice to be occupationally competent in accordance with the occupational standards marking schemes and grading descriptors - the details of which are stated in the end-point assessment plan linked specifically to each occupational standard.\n\n18\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: requirement
  rung: none
  force: must
  level: none
  appliesTo: "independent assessors of degree apprenticeships"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["ensure", "involved", "receive", "deem"]
  about: "independent assessment of apprentices"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["degree apprenticeship"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Independent assessment judges an apprentice's occupational competence through assessors with no stake in the result who are respected experts; professional bodies acting as independent providers will keep it independent, the assessor must not have trained or employed the apprentice and, in England, must gain nothing from the result and must judge competence only by the occupational standard's marking and grading set out in the EPA plan."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/4.2
  locator: { number: "4.2", headingPath: ["Benchmark standards", "Introduction"] }
  span: { start: 55017, end: 55502 }
  source: "4.2 The vast majority of students will perform significantly better than the minimum threshold standards. Each higher education provider has its own method of determining what appropriate evidence of this achievement will be and should refer to Annex D in The Frameworks for Higher Education Qualifications of UK Degree-Awarding Bodies. This Annex sets out common descriptions of the four main degree outcome classifications for bachelor's degrees with honours: 1st, 2.1, 2.2 and 3rd.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: fheq:6
  appliesTo: "higher education providers"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["refer"]
  about: "evidence above the threshold and Annex D"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Most students will perform well above the threshold; each provider decides what evidence shows this and should refer to Annex D of the Frameworks, which describes the four honours classes."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited

  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }

- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.12
  forPractitioner: { text: "Course design, teaching and assessment should be adapted to widen inclusion, whether through technology or simulated experience, with attention to the most effective methods that are affordable.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 1.12 as a provider expectation (should) about inclusive design, teaching and assessment, with no level attached. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 1.12. Do not claim that it requires any particular technology. Set it beside 1.8, 2.9 (adjustable assessments) and 3.17 (accessible assessment and alternatives)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team plans module delivery or assessment and asks how to make it inclusive. Frame it for staff as a design choice they should make from the start, using tools or simulation where these help. Ask: 'Which part of this module would a student with a barrier to conventional learning find hardest, and what would remove it?' Avoid presenting it to students as their task."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.13
  forPractitioner: { text: "Graduates should leave able to build EDI and accessibility into their professional work and to weigh, across a project's life, how it affects end users from diverse populations.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "You should be able to build equality, diversity, inclusion and accessibility into your work as a professional, and to think through how a project, from start to finish, affects the many different people who will use it.", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 1.13 as an expectation (should) of honours graduates that carries the EDI theme into professional practice, at FHEQ level 6. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 1.13. Do not claim that it is a rung of achievement or a legal standard. Set it beside 1.8 to 1.12, the threshold items on inclusive environments (4.3.b68, 4.3.b143, 4.3.b168) and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b9.1 (spine:6)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a student or team discusses inclusion in projects, placements or professional conduct. Tell a student in plain words that they should be ready to plan and run projects with every end user in mind; for staff, frame it as an outcome to build into project work. Ask: 'Who might be left out by the project you are working on, and how would you know?' Avoid reading the paragraph aloud or treating it as a rung above the threshold."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.14
  forPractitioner: { text: "Graduates should respond to sustainability challenges through ethical conduct that protects quality of life, communities and the planet, weighing today's environmental, economic and social needs against those of the future, and keep to those values once qualified.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "You should respond to challenges such as climate change by acting ethically and protecting people, communities and the planet, weighing what people need now against what future generations will need, and keep to those values once you are qualified.", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 1.14 as an expectation (should) of honours graduates under the sustainability theme, at FHEQ level 6. Set it beside 1.15 to 1.18, the content lists in 3.4 and 3.5, the threshold items 4.3.b70 and 4.3.b165 and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b9.1 (spine:6)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a student asks what sustainability means for their future practice, or a team plans sustainability content. Put it to a student as the values you should carry into your work; for staff, as a graduate expectation the course should build towards. - statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.15
  forPractitioner: { text: "Courses should also give attention to the UK's statement on the SDGs and to the four nations' agendas, including those of stakeholders such as RICS and CIOB, while students explore how they could contribute to the UN goals and the HABITAT Agenda.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "You can explore how your work might help meet the UN Sustainable Development Goals and the HABITAT Agenda, and you should also look at how the UK and its four nations, and bodies such as RICS and CIOB, approach them.", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 1.15 as an expectation (should) that the UK and national SDG agendas be considered, beside an open invitation to explore the UN goals. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 1.15. Do not claim that it requires any particular goal to be taught. Set it beside 1.14, 1.16 and the ESD guidance cited there (std:doc:qaa-advancehe-esd-2021-guidance)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a student or team asks how global sustainability goals connect to the course. Put it simply: the international goals give a frame, and the UK and its nations have their own versions to look at too. Ask: 'Which of the goals does your project touch, and what do the UK's or your nation's targets say about it?' Avoid presenting the invitation to explore as a requirement on students."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.16
  forPractitioner: { text: "Sustainability theory and practice should run through the curriculum and its assessment in a balanced, innovative way; align it with the ESD Guidance outcomes, develop subject and transferable skills evenly, and consider the UNESCO competencies, such as systems thinking and critical reflection.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 1.16 as a provider expectation (should) for sustainability in curriculum and assessment, with a further 'shall' on balancing skills (Part E). Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 1.16. Do not claim that the UNESCO competencies are required, since the issuer says courses can adopt them. Set it beside 1.14, 1.17, 3.18 and the ESD guidance (std:doc:qaa-advancehe-esd-2021-guidance)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team designs or reviews how sustainability is taught and assessed. Frame it for staff as an expectation that sustainability is assessed, not only taught, with the guidance's outcomes as a check. - statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.18
  forPractitioner: { text: "Learning opportunities on sustainability should be designed to build ethical behaviour and ESG values that match accreditation requirements and industry good practice, raising students' awareness of corporate responsibility.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 1.18 as a provider expectation (should) on ethics and ESG values in sustainability learning. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 1.18.   author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team links sustainability teaching to professional accreditation or employer needs. Frame it for staff as a design aim: build ethical behaviour and ESG values, not just knowledge of them. - statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.21
  forPractitioner: { text: "The course will prepare students for changing business conditions and rapid technological change, with real or simulated placements and activities that connect it to external organisations and build commercial and corporate awareness.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a statement of what courses will provide; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 1.21 as a provider expectation (will) under the employability, enterprise and entrepreneurship theme. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 1.21.   author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team plans placements, simulations or links with employers. - statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.26
  forPractitioner: { text: "The course will give students working use of digital tools such as BIM and AI for gathering, handling, storing and sharing data.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "You will work with digital tools, among them building information modelling and AI, to gather, handle, store and share data.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 1.26 as a course expectation (will) under the information management theme. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 1.26. Do not claim that any one software product is required. Set it beside 1.27, 1.28.b1 to 1.28.b3 and the digital literacy threshold items 4.3.b33 to 4.3.b36."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a student asks which digital tools they will learn, or a team plans software in the curriculum. Tell a student plainly that work with tools such as BIM and AI is part of the course; for staff, frame it as an expected feature of delivery. Ask: 'Which digital tools have you used on real data so far, and for what?' Avoid promising particular software the course does not offer."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.28.b1
  forPractitioner: { text: "Graduates should be competent with electronic information and data processing software, interpret digital data to support decisions, work to standards such as ISO 19650 with tools such as BIM, organise records digitally and understand data protection and security.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "You should be able to handle electronic information and process data with the right software, use digital data to support decisions, work to standards such as ISO 19650 with tools such as BIM, keep information and records organised and protect data security and privacy.", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 1.28 as an expectation (should) of honours graduates in the first of three information management areas (1.28.b1), at FHEQ level 6. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 1.28. Do not claim that ISO 19650 or BIM is mandatory in every course, since they are given as examples. Set it beside 1.28.b2, 1.28.b3, the threshold items 4.3.b35 and 4.3.b89 and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b2 (spine:6)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a student asks what information management skills employers expect, or a team maps digital content. Put it to a student as the everyday competence you should have with digital information and records; for staff, as an expected graduate competence. Ask: 'Can you describe how you organised and secured the data on your last project, and which standard you followed?' Avoid reading the long paragraph aloud."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.28.b2
  forPractitioner: { text: "Graduates will know and apply digital technologies relevant to their field, such as BIM, and use digital tools in core subjects; they should understand how AI can cut errors and repetitive work across the project life cycle.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "You will know and use the digital technologies of your field, such as BIM, and you should understand how AI can make projects more efficient by reducing errors and taking on repetitive tasks.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 1.28 as an expectation (will, with should and an informal 'a must' inside) of honours graduates in the second information management area (1.28.b2), at FHEQ level 6. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 1.28. Do not claim that the issuer's 'a must' creates a formal requirement (Part E). Set it beside 1.28.b1, 1.28.b3, the threshold item 4.3.b90 and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b2 (spine:6)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a student asks how AI and BIM will affect their work, or staff plan digital teaching. Explain in plain words that you will be expected to use your field's digital tools and to understand what AI can do for a project. Ask: 'Where could AI remove a repetitive task in a project you know, and what could go wrong if it did?' Avoid overstating what AI does or reading the issuer's text aloud."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/1.28.b3
  forPractitioner: { text: "Graduates should know the limits of online platforms as a channel and a source, communicate through varied ICT platforms and social media to different audiences, and use digital technology lawfully, safely and ethically.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "You should know where online platforms fall short as a way to communicate or find information, use different devices and social media to reach different audiences, and use digital technology safely, ethically and within the law.", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 1.28 as an expectation (should) of honours graduates in the third information management area (1.28.b3), at FHEQ level 6. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 1.28. Do not claim that it covers generative AI in detail, which the threshold item 4.3.b36 does. Set it beside 1.28.b1, 1.28.b2, the digital literacy threshold items 4.3.b33 to 4.3.b36 and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b8 (spine:6)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a student asks about using social media or online sources professionally. Put it plainly to a student: you should judge online sources and channels critically and use them safely. Ask: 'How did you check the online source you used last, and who was your message for?' Avoid lecturing or reading the paragraph aloud."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/2.4
  forPractitioner: { text: "The course should be designed so that communication, critical thinking, problem-solving, self-management and data handling, together with teamwork, develop step by step alongside subject competencies.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 2.4 as a provider expectation (should) on course design for transferable skills. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 2.4. Do not claim that it lists outcomes; the outcomes are the threshold items in 4.3. Set it beside 3.7, 3.8 and the generic threshold lists 4.3.b9 to 4.3.b53."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team maps where transferable skills are taught across the years. Frame it for staff as a design expectation to grow these skills progressively, not in one module. Ask: 'In which year is teamwork first assessed, and how does it build after that?' Avoid giving the design duty to a student."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/2.7
  forPractitioner: { text: "Accessibility for prospective and current students is an obligation on every provider, and the EDI expectations set earlier (1.8 to 1.13) apply to all courses in the field.", rewrite: light, force: must, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: must, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 2.7 as a provider obligation, recorded as 'describes' because the issuer's word 'obligation' is outside the five force words (Part E). Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 2.7. Do not claim that it adds legal duties of its own. Set it beside 1.8 to 1.13, 2.9, 2.10 and 3.17."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team reviews the accessibility of admissions, delivery or assessment. Frame it for staff as an obligation on the whole course, not an optional extra. Ask: 'What would a disabled applicant find hardest about joining this course, and who owns fixing it?' Avoid putting the obligation to a student."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/2.9
  forPractitioner: { text: "Learning outcomes and their assessment should be stated clearly; assessments should be designed so they can be adjusted for an individual's needs under institutional guidelines, and students with adjustments should be able to reach support services.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 2.9 as a provider expectation (should) on assessment design and reasonable adjustment. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 2.9. Do not claim that it overrides institutional guidelines, which it defers to. Set it beside 1.12, 2.7, 3.17 and 3.19."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team designs assessments or handles requests for adjustment. Frame it for staff: outcomes and assessment should be explicit, and assessments built so they can flex for a student who needs it. Ask: 'If a student needed this assessment changed, which part could change without losing the outcome?' Avoid treating adjustment as a favour rather than an expectation."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/2.14
  forPractitioner: { text: "Plan for every student to complete a work-related project, a dissertation or both, supported by industry input such as live projects, guest lectures, case studies and placements.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "You will be expected to complete a work-related project, a dissertation or both, and you may meet industry through guest lectures, live projects, case studies or a placement.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 2.14 as an expectation (will) that students complete a work-related project, a dissertation or both in the honours degree. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 2.14. Do not claim that both are required, since the issuer allows either. Set it beside 2.13, 2.17, 3.14 and the practical threshold item 4.3.b18."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a student asks about the final-year project or a team plans industry links. Tell a student plainly that a project tied to work, a dissertation or both will be part of the course. Ask: 'What kind of project would connect your interests with a real employer's problem?' Avoid implying that a placement is guaranteed."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/2.22
  forPractitioner: { text: "Apprenticeship delivery must be planned and monitored jointly with employers and apprentices for its whole length, with on- and off-the-job training joined up, and staff must teach up-to-date vocational and technical knowledge.", rewrite: light, force: must, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: must, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 2.22 as a requirement (must) on providers delivering degree apprenticeships across the UK. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 2.22. Do not claim that it sets the apprenticeship standard itself, which is the occupational standard. Set it beside 2.23 to 2.25 and 3.21."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team sets up or reviews a degree apprenticeship. Frame it for staff as a firm requirement to plan and monitor training with employers and apprentices. Ask: 'How do on- and off-the-job training link in your plan, and how often do you review it with the employer?' Avoid giving the requirement to an apprentice as their duty."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/2.23
  forPractitioner: { text: "For integrated degree apprenticeships in England, the award must fully meet the occupational standard and its EPA plan, and the provider must follow that plan in full to secure independent assessment of competence.", rewrite: light, force: must, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: must, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 2.23 as a requirement (must) on providers of integrated degree apprenticeships in England. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 2.23. Do not claim that it applies in Wales, Scotland or Northern Ireland. Set it beside 2.22, 2.24, 2.25 and 3.21."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team in England plans the end-point assessment of an integrated apprenticeship. Frame it for staff as a condition of the award: the EPA plan is followed in full. Ask: 'Which part of the EPA plan is the provider's responsibility, and how is independence protected?' Avoid applying it outside England."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/2.24
  forPractitioner: { text: "Suitably qualified assessors for independent EPAs must be found and trained (industry practitioners can serve); the degree certificate of an integrated apprenticeship is issued only after the EPA is passed.", rewrite: light, force: must, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider; the condition on the certificate follows from 2.23", rewrite: full, force: must, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 2.24 as a requirement on providers in England, read as must from 'will need to' (Part E), with a condition on awarding the degree. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 2.24. Do not claim that the degree can be awarded before the EPA is passed. Set it beside 2.23, 2.25 and 3.21."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team in England prepares assessors or plans the timing of awards for apprentices. Frame it for staff: assessors must be found and trained, and no certificate goes out before the EPA is passed. Ask: 'Who will act as independent assessors, and how will you show they are competent?' Avoid giving the duty to an apprentice."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/2.25
  forPractitioner: { text: "For non-integrated apprenticeships in England, the provider must deliver the degree named in the occupational standard and know that finishing it may be a prerequisite for the EPA gateway, with the EPA itself run by an external organisation.", rewrite: light, force: must, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: must, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 2.25 as a requirement (must) on providers of non-integrated degree apprenticeships in England. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 2.25. Do not claim that the provider runs the end-point assessment. Set it beside 2.22 to 2.24 and 3.21."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team in England plans a non-integrated apprenticeship. Frame it for staff: deliver the named degree, and plan for the chance that it must be finished before the EPA gateway. Ask: 'Does your occupational standard require the degree to be complete before the gateway, and is your timetable built for that?' Avoid applying it outside England."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.6.b1
  forPractitioner: { text: "By graduation, students of the subject will be able to show familiarity with a wide range of the subject's facts and principles while aware of where current theory and applied knowledge stop; plan where the course develops and assesses it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "By the end of your degree you will be able to show familiarity with a wide range of the subject's facts and principles while aware of where current theory and applied knowledge stop.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Present item 1 of 3.6 as an outcome of honours study in the subject, not a rung: graduates are able to do this by the award, at FHEQ level 6 (FQHEIS level 10), here on familiarity with subject facts and the limits of theory. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.6 (item 1). Do not call it a regulatory requirement or a target for the typical student. Set it beside 3.6.b2, the generic threshold lists in 4.3 and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 (spine:6)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise it when a student or course team asks what graduates of the subject can do on familiarity with subject facts and the limits of theory. Say it plainly to a student, for example: by the end of the degree you will be able to show familiarity with a wide range of the subject's facts and principles while aware of where current theory and applied knowledge stop; with staff, put it as an outcome to map to modules. Ask: 'Where have you done this so far, and what evidence would show it?' Avoid reading the issuer's list aloud."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.6.b2
  forPractitioner: { text: "By graduation, students of the subject will be able to understand that the way a problem is defined, and the information around it, is provisional, and leave room for competing explanations in the subject; plan where the course develops and assesses it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "By the end of your degree you will be able to understand that the way a problem is defined, and the information around it, is provisional, and leave room for competing explanations in the subject.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Present item 2 of 3.6 as an outcome of honours study in the subject, not a rung: graduates are able to do this by the award, at FHEQ level 6 (FQHEIS level 10), here on provisional problem definition and rival explanations. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.6 (item 2). Do not call it a regulatory requirement or a target for the typical student. Set it beside 3.6.b1 and 3.6.b3, the generic threshold lists in 4.3 and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b4 (spine:6)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise it when a student or course team asks what graduates of the subject can do on provisional problem definition and rival explanations. - statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.6.b3
  forPractitioner: { text: "By graduation, students of the subject will be able to show understanding of what defines the subject, gained through in-depth or cross-curricular study or research; plan where the course develops and assesses it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "By the end of your degree you will be able to show understanding of what defines the subject, gained through in-depth or cross-curricular study or research.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Present item 3 of 3.6 as an outcome of honours study in the subject, not a rung: graduates are able to do this by the award, at FHEQ level 6 (FQHEIS level 10), here on the subject's defining elements.   author-agent-dialogue: "Raise it when a student or course team asks what graduates of the subject can do on the subject's defining elements.
