```yaml
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.6.b4
  forPractitioner: { text: "By graduation, students of the subject will be able to tackle problems by gathering, analysing and judging qualitative and quantitative information, then using it inventively to solve problems, introduce innovations and take decisions through to completion; plan where the course develops and assesses it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "By the end of your degree you will be able to tackle problems by gathering, analysing and judging qualitative and quantitative information, then using it inventively to solve problems, introduce innovations and take decisions through to completion.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Present item 4 of 3.6 as an outcome of honours study in the subject, not a rung: graduates are able to do this by the award, at FHEQ level 6 (FQHEIS level 10), here on creative problem-solving and decision-making. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.6 (item 4). Do not call it a regulatory requirement or a target for the typical student. Set it beside 3.6.b3 and 3.6.b5, the generic threshold lists in 4.3 and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b7 (spine:6)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise it when a student or course team asks what graduates of the subject can do on creative problem-solving and decision-making. Say it plainly to a student, for example: by the end of the degree you will be able to tackle problems by gathering, analysing and judging qualitative and quantitative information, then using it inventively to solve problems, introduce innovations and take decisions through to completion; with staff, put it as an outcome to map to modules. Ask: 'Where have you done this so far, and what evidence would show it?' Avoid reading the issuer's list aloud."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.6.b5
  forPractitioner: { text: "By graduation, students of the subject will be able to plan and carry out research or development work, judge its outcomes, reach sound conclusions and make recommendations; plan where the course develops and assesses it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "By the end of your degree you will be able to plan and carry out research or development work, judge its outcomes, reach sound conclusions and make recommendations.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Present item 5 of 3.6 as an outcome of honours study in the subject, not a rung: graduates are able to do this by the award, at FHEQ level 6 (FQHEIS level 10), here on research or development work. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.6 (item 5). Do not call it a regulatory requirement or a target for the typical student. Set it beside 3.6.b4 and 3.6.b6, the generic threshold lists in 4.3 and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b6 (spine:6)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise it when a student or course team asks what graduates of the subject can do on research or development work. Say it plainly to a student, for example: by the end of the degree you will be able to plan and carry out research or development work, judge its outcomes, reach sound conclusions and make recommendations; with staff, put it as an outcome to map to modules. Ask: 'Where have you done this so far, and what evidence would show it?' Avoid reading the issuer's list aloud."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.6.b6
  forPractitioner: { text: "By graduation, students of the subject will be able to evaluate and interpret, even-handedly, new information that others supply from a range of fields; plan where the course develops and assesses it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "By the end of your degree you will be able to evaluate and interpret, even-handedly, new information that others supply from a range of fields.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Present item 6 of 3.6 as an outcome of honours study in the subject, not a rung: graduates are able to do this by the award, at FHEQ level 6 (FQHEIS level 10), here on evaluating information from other fields. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.6 (item 6). Do not call it a regulatory requirement or a target for the typical student. Set it beside 3.6.b5 and 3.6.b7, the generic threshold lists in 4.3 and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b7 (spine:6)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise it when a student or course team asks what graduates of the subject can do on evaluating information from other fields. Say it plainly to a student, for example: by the end of the degree you will be able to evaluate and interpret, even-handedly, new information that others supply from a range of fields; with staff, put it as an outcome to map to modules. Ask: 'Where have you done this so far, and what evidence would show it?' Avoid reading the issuer's list aloud."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.6.b7
  forPractitioner: { text: "By graduation, students of the subject will be able to show general scholarly skills and professional skills specific to their award, and the ability to pick up the new competencies a career needs; plan where the course develops and assesses it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "By the end of your degree you will be able to show general scholarly skills and professional skills specific to your award, and the ability to pick up the new competencies a career needs.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Present item 7 of 3.6 as an outcome of honours study in the subject, not a rung: graduates are able to do this by the award, at FHEQ level 6 (FQHEIS level 10), here on scholarly and professional skills and new competencies. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.6 (item 7). Do not call it a regulatory requirement or a target for the typical student. Set it beside 3.6.b6 and 3.6.b8, the generic threshold lists in 4.3 and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b9.3 (spine:6)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise it when a student or course team asks what graduates of the subject can do on scholarly and professional skills and new competencies. Say it plainly to a student, for example: by the end of the degree you will be able to show general scholarly skills and professional skills specific to your award, and the ability to pick up the new competencies a career needs; with staff, put it as an outcome to map to modules. Ask: 'Where have you done this so far, and what evidence would show it?' Avoid reading the issuer's list aloud."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.6.b8
  forPractitioner: { text: "By graduation, students of the subject will be able to recognise the limits of their knowledge and skills and know when, how and where to get authoritative advice; plan where the course develops and assesses it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "By the end of your degree you will be able to recognise the limits of your knowledge and skills and know when, how and where to get authoritative advice.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Present item 8 of 3.6 as an outcome of honours study in the subject, not a rung: graduates are able to do this by the award, at FHEQ level 6 (FQHEIS level 10), here on limits of own knowledge and seeking advice. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.6 (item 8). Do not call it a regulatory requirement or a target for the typical student. Set it beside 3.6.b7 and 3.6.b9, the generic threshold lists in 4.3 and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b4 (spine:6)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise it when a student or course team asks what graduates of the subject can do on limits of own knowledge and seeking advice. Say it plainly to a student, for example: by the end of the degree you will be able to recognise the limits of your knowledge and skills and know when, how and where to get authoritative advice; with staff, put it as an outcome to map to modules. Ask: 'Where have you done this so far, and what evidence would show it?' Avoid reading the issuer's list aloud."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.6.b9
  forPractitioner: { text: "By graduation, students of the subject will be able to weigh the effects of human activity on ethics, equality and inclusion, so that communities and the environment can be sustained as well as possible; plan where the course develops and assesses it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "By the end of your degree you will be able to weigh the effects of human activity on ethics, equality and inclusion, so that communities and the environment can be sustained as well as possible.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Present item 9 of 3.6 as an outcome of honours study in the subject, not a rung: graduates are able to do this by the award, at FHEQ level 6 (FQHEIS level 10), here on ethical and equality consequences for sustainability. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.6 (item 9). Do not call it a regulatory requirement or a target for the typical student. Set it beside 3.6.b8, the generic threshold lists in 4.3 and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b9.2 (spine:6)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise it when a student or course team asks what graduates of the subject can do on ethical and equality consequences for sustainability. Say it plainly to a student, for example: by the end of the degree you will be able to weigh the effects of human activity on ethics, equality and inclusion, so that communities and the environment can be sustained as well as possible; with staff, put it as an outcome to map to modules. Ask: 'Where have you done this so far, and what evidence would show it?' Avoid reading the issuer's list aloud."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.9
  forPractitioner: { text: "Teaching and assessment are best aligned with module outcomes; practice should support conceptual understanding, applied knowledge and reflection on performance, and should build resilience and a habit of lifelong learning.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 3.9 as a provider expectation (should) on the aims of learning, teaching and assessment. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.9. Do not claim that constructive alignment is required; the issuer says it is favoured. Set it beside 3.10 to 3.19."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team reviews its teaching approach or module alignment. Frame it for staff: the practices should build understanding, application, reflection and resilience. Ask: 'Where in the module do students reflect on their own performance, and is it assessed?' Avoid presenting it as a student duty."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.10
  forPractitioner: { text: "Teaching should fit each learning setting and delivery mode should fit the material; where suitable, practice should build in inclusive practice, sustainable development, employability and information management skills.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 3.10 as a provider expectation (should) tying delivery to the cross-cutting themes. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.10. Do not claim that each theme needs a separate module; the issuer says to embed them where appropriate. Set it beside 1.8 to 1.28, 3.9 and 3.11."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team maps how the themes appear in delivery. Frame it for staff as an expectation to weave the four named themes into ordinary teaching. Ask: 'In which sessions do inclusion, sustainability, employability and information management actually show up?' Avoid presenting it to students as their task."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.11
  forPractitioner: { text: "Teaching should develop applied knowledge and skills from the programme outcomes and help students link modules critically and set their learning in the sector, using live projects, case studies, simulations and collaboration.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 3.11 as a provider expectation (should) on integrative, applied teaching. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.11. Do not claim that the listed methods are required; the issuer encourages them. Set it beside 3.9, 3.12 and 3.19."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team plans integrative or project-based teaching. Frame it for staff: students should be helped to join modules up and apply them in sector contexts. Ask: 'Which assessment asks students to draw on two or more modules at once?' Avoid giving it to students as a rule."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.12
  forPractitioner: { text: "Teaching should take account of students' different starting points and modes of study, with practice-based scenarios where work-based learning is not available.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 3.12 as a provider expectation (should) on starting points and study modes. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.12. Do not claim that scenarios replace work-based learning where it exists. Set it beside 2.13, 3.11 and 3.13."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team teaches mixed cohorts, such as apprentices beside full-time students. Frame it for staff: the pedagogy should flex to where students start from. Ask: 'How does the module serve a part-time employed learner differently from a full-time one?' Avoid presenting it as a student duty."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.13
  forPractitioner: { text: "A range of teaching approaches should be used, face to face and online, so that different learning preferences are served.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 3.13 as a provider expectation (should) on variety in teaching. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.13. Do not claim that it endorses any one theory of learning styles. Set it beside 2.9, 3.12 and 3.14."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team reviews the mix of teaching methods. Frame it for staff: variety in teaching should be planned, in person and online. Ask: 'How many different ways does a student meet the key idea in this module?' Avoid presenting it to students as a requirement."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.14
  forPractitioner: { text: "Learning should be supported by research-informed as well as practice-informed teaching, in any mix of research-led, -oriented, -tutored or -based learning, which can include independent research projects or dissertations.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 3.14 as a provider expectation (should) on research-informed teaching. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.14. Do not claim that every student must write a dissertation; 2.14 allows a work-related project instead. Set it beside 2.14, 3.6.b5 and the practical threshold item 4.3.b18."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team plans research skills or final projects. Frame it for staff: teaching should draw on research as well as practice. Ask: 'Where do students meet current research in this module, and do they do any themselves?' Avoid presenting it as a student duty."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.15
  forPractitioner: { text: "Teaching should actively foster integrity in academic work and in professional conduct.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 3.15 as a provider expectation (should) on integrity. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.15. Do not claim that it sets a misconduct procedure. Set it beside 3.16 and the threshold items 4.3.b17 and 4.3.b53."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team plans how integrity is taught. Frame it for staff: integrity should be taught, not only policed. Ask: 'Where do students practise acting with integrity before they are assessed on it?' Avoid using it to warn students rather than teach them."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.16
  forPractitioner: { text: "Generative AI should be planned into authentic learning to mirror workplace practice; where AI supports learning, deep learning and critical thinking should be protected, and its ethical issues (data quality, sustainability, integrity, inclusion and wider social effects) addressed.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 3.16 as a provider expectation (should) on generative AI in learning. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.16. Do not claim that it bans or requires AI tools. Set it beside 3.15, the digital literacy threshold item 4.3.b36 and 1.28.b2."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team sets a policy or tasks for generative AI. Frame it for staff: AI use should be planned for authentic tasks while keeping critical thinking in front. Ask: 'In this assessment, what does the student still have to think through without AI?' Avoid presenting it to students as a rule they must follow."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.17
  forPractitioner: { text: "For each assessment method the aims, what it requires and its marking criteria should be clear; assessment should be accessible, with alternatives offered where appropriate.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 3.17 as a provider expectation (should) on the clarity and accessibility of assessment. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.17. Do not claim that alternatives are owed in every case; the issuer says where appropriate. Set it beside 1.12, 2.9, 3.18 and the Annex D descriptions cited in 4.2."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team writes assessment briefs or criteria. Frame it for staff: aims, requirements and criteria should be explicit for every method. Ask: 'Could a student tell from this brief exactly how the work will be marked?' Avoid putting the duty to the student."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.18
  forPractitioner: { text: "Assessment should rest on the Quality Code's Assessment guidance, use a wide range of methods to measure achievement of outcomes, and include assessment for, as and of learning, with self, peer and practitioner assessment where it helps.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 3.18 as a provider expectation (should) on assessment principles and range. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.18. Do not claim that self, peer or practitioner assessment is required; it is encouraged. Set it beside 3.17, 3.19, 3.20 and the ESD guidance (std:doc:qaa-advancehe-esd-2021-guidance)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team reviews its assessment strategy. Frame it for staff: the range of methods should be wide enough to show every outcome. Ask: 'Which outcomes are only ever assessed by one method?' Avoid presenting it to students as their duty."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.19
  forPractitioner: { text: "Assessment should be designed so students see its worth beyond university, using authentic tasks and scenarios tied to module outcomes, such as professional roles, and should allow tasks to be localised.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 3.19 as a provider expectation (should) on authentic assessment. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.19. Do not claim that every task must be work-based. Set it beside 3.11, 3.18 and 3.20."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team designs assessments or asks how to make them authentic. Frame it for staff: a student should be able to see why the task matters beyond the course. Ask: 'If a graduate did this task at work, who would the client be?' Avoid presenting it as a student duty."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/3.21
  forPractitioner: { text: "Independent assessment must stay independent: the assessor must not have trained or employed the apprentice and, in England, must have nothing to gain or lose from the result and must judge competence only against the EPA plan's marking and grading.", rewrite: light, force: must, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: must, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 3.21 as a requirement (must) on independent assessors of degree apprenticeships. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.21. Do not claim that the England-only conditions apply in the other nations. Set it beside 2.22 to 2.25."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team chooses assessors or reviews an apprenticeship EPA. Frame it for staff: independence is a firm condition, and the assessor's judgement rests on the EPA plan alone. Ask: 'Has anyone proposed as assessor had a hand in this apprentice's training or employment?' Avoid telling the apprentice it is their responsibility."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/4.2
  forPractitioner: { text: "When judging performance above the threshold, a provider uses its own method of evidence and should refer to Annex D's descriptions of the four honours classifications.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat paragraph 4.2 as a provider expectation (should) to use Annex D for performance above the threshold. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2. Do not claim that the statement itself sets typical or excellent rungs; it does not. Set it beside 4.1, the threshold items in 4.3 and the Annex D descriptions (qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/summary/r1/3rd and the other columns in std:doc:qaa-fheq-2024-framework, SL6)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring it up when a team asks how to grade above the minimum or what a 2.1 looks like. Frame it for staff: the threshold is the floor, and the classification descriptions in Annex D guide what lies above it. Ask: 'How do your marking criteria tell a 2.2 from a 2.1 on this outcome?' Avoid presenting the threshold as what most students achieve."
- statement: qaa/land-construction-real-estate-and-surveying-2024/4.3.b9
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the threshold for the honours degree, every graduate will be able to analyse information from varied sources critically, then draw it together and condense it; the course needs teaching that builds this and assessment that shows it before the award.", rewrite: light, force: threshold, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will be able to analyse information from varied sources critically, then draw it together and condense it.", rewrite: full, force: threshold, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Present this as a threshold outcome of the bachelor's degree with honours, at FHEQ level 6 (FQHEIS level 10): the minimum every graduate shows by the award, here on critical analysis of information. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.3 (Table 1, intellectual skills, item 1). Do not call it a regulatory requirement or the level most students reach, and do not stretch it to master's study. Set it beside 4.3.b10 and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b7 (spine:6)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise it when a student or course team asks what a pass in any course in the subject demands on critical analysis of information. Say it plainly to a student, for example: by the end of the degree you need to be able to analyse information from varied sources critically, then draw it together and condense it; with staff, put it as an outcome the course needs to teach and assess. Ask: 'Where have you done this so far, and what evidence would show it?' Avoid reading the issuer's list aloud and avoid treating this minimum as the level most students reach."

  author-agent-written: "Present this as a threshold outcome of the bachelor's degree with honours in planning and development, at FHEQ level 6 (FQHEIS level 10): the minimum every graduate shows by the award, here on surveying a development site. Cite it as QAA (2024), Land, Construction, Real Estate and Surveying Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.3 (Table 1, Planning and Development, item 1). Do not call it a regulatory requirement or the level most students reach, and do not stretch it to master's study. Set it beside 4.3.b146 and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b2 (spine:6)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise it when a student or course team asks what a pass in planning and development demands on surveying a development site. Say it plainly to a student, for example: by the end of the degree you need to be able to assess land by surveying, measuring and analysing it, and judge its potential as a site for development; with staff, put it as an outcome the course needs to teach and assess. Ask: 'Where have you done this so far, and what evidence would show it?' Avoid reading the issuer's list aloud and avoid treating this minimum as the level most students reach."

  forPractitioner: { text: "At the threshold for the honours degree in planning and development, every graduate will be able to evaluate the character, history, geography and dynamics of a site's built and natural heritage, and how far planning documents support converting the land; the course needs teaching that builds this and assessment that shows it before the award.", rewrite: light, force: threshold, status: drafted }

  forPractitioner: { text: "At the threshold for the honours degree in planning and development, every graduate will be able to assess how the real estate market fixes a development site's value, what valuation and analysis methods exist for that appraisal, and run a sensitivity analysis on the risk of conversion; the course needs teaching that builds this and assessment that shows it before the award.", rewrite: light, force: threshold, status: drafted }
```

### S9 Commentary (optional)
<!-- label: editorial; status: drafted; basis: secondary -->
`unknown`: not researched in P1 (Part E).

### SB1 The subject
<!-- label: both; status: drafted; basis: primary -->
- A group of built environment subjects, land, construction, real estate and surveying, holistic and interdisciplinary, working beside architecture, planning, housing and allied professions (D1, 1.1)
- Strongly vocational, preparing for professional careers with or without accreditation by bodies such as RICS, CIOB, CICES and CABE (D1, 1.3, 1.7, 2.1)
- Eight indicative categories, from building surveying and quantity surveying to rural land and geospatial surveying (D1, 1.4)
- Courses sit at different points of the land, construction and real estate life cycle: decisions to convert land, physical change, stewardship of buildings and management of assets (D1, 2.5)
- Links theory to practice, building competence and capability, with transferable skills grown beside subject competencies (D1, 2.4, 3.9)
- Close ties with employers and professional bodies, including degree apprenticeships (D1, 1.24, 2.14, 2.22 to 2.25)
- An early adopter of digital technology, with information management, BIM and AI central (D1, 1.26 to 1.28, 2.10)
- Shapes how people live and an inclusive, sustainable built and natural environment, often internationally (D1, 2.1, 2.6)

### SB2 Themes in this subject
<!-- label: both; status: drafted; basis: primary -->

## Part E Gaps and questions
- Rungs: threshold only. Table 1 (4.3) holds seven generic lists and nine subject lists for the honours degree; 4.2 hands performance above threshold to Annex D; there are no typical or excellent lists, so no gradeOf links. Different from Psychology.
- Master's: 4.4 is a master's list (4 items), recorded as rung threshold, award master's, level fheq:7. The ids keep the skeleton's 4.4.b1 to b4 rather than a `.masters` suffix, since 4.4 is its own paragraph with its own span: for std to rule.
- SB4: the two threshold rows (honours 156, master's 4) together equal the 160 records with rung threshold.
- Lead-in wording: "To meet the threshold, graduates will be able to" (subject lists), "Graduates of courses covered by this Statement are able to" (generic lists), "will be able to demonstrate:" with noun phrases (Quantity Surveying, Construction Management), "demonstrate knowledge and understanding relating to:" (Rural Land) and "demonstrate knowledge, understanding, skills and competencies in:" (Geospatial). Force is threshold from 4.1, which defines the threshold as the minimum for the award (forceFrom 4.1; 4.4 for the master's list).
- Noun-phrase items (Quantity Surveying, Construction Management, Rural Land, Geospatial) take the lead-in verb "demonstrate", which lies outside their spans: `verbs` holds only verbs inside each span and is empty where there are none.
- 4.3 lists eight subject categories (and 1.4 the same eight), but Table 1 has nine subject lists: Real Estate and Real Estate Finance and Investment are separate lists under one category.
- Skeleton: 4.3.b1 to b8 are the names of the eight categories, not statements; dropped.
- Skeleton: 4.3.b107 to b111 are sub-items of b106 (the costs to weigh in fee quotations); folded into b106 by `find`.
- Skeleton: spans extended across page breaks by `find` for 1.28.b1 (ended at "the need for"), 4.3.b95 ("(BSR) under the HSE" after a break) and 4.3.b140 (its second half after page 30).
- Skeleton: the issuer's 2.2 has no number in the held text; its matter ("Professional body accreditation is integral...") runs on inside 2.1 with no space after "needs.". Kept inside 2.1 (gloss says so); check against the original PDF.
- Not taken as statements: the nine subject-list introductions ("Graduates have some familiarity with...", "They also appreciate..."), written in the plain present. The Geospatial one opens "To meet the threshold, graduates have a basic understanding of..." and the Quantity Surveying one ends in the lead-in; for Abbott at P3 whether these introductions are threshold statements.
- Obliging words outside the five: "shall" (1.16, "shall be evenly cultivated"; Planning and Development introduction, "shall have regard"); "obligation" (2.7, recorded as describes); "duty" (1.8, beside should); "are expected to" (1.13 and 1.14, beside should; 4.5, recorded as describes); "will need to" (2.24, read as must, the meaning being plain); "it is expected that" (2.11), "there is an expectation that" (2.14, beside will), "it is anticipated that" (2.13); "a must for graduates" (1.28.b2, informal, inside a will paragraph). For Abbott.
- Plain-present provider norms, not statements (template open point 5): 1.11 ("all students are provided with the support"), 3.4 (features all courses share), 3.5 ("Each course addresses:", nine items), 3.20 (assessment types "likely to include"), 2.18 and 2.19 (providers draw on reference points; use external reviewers).
- Predictive or descriptive "will" not taken: 1.10 ("This will ensure consideration of EDI"), 2.10, 2.11 ("students ... will progress"), 2.18, 2.20. Taken: 1.21 and 1.26 ("Courses will ..."), read as provider expectations.
- 1.28: its lead sentence ("Graduates should be competent in ...") is carried by its three area records (1.28.b1 to b3), not recorded on its own.
- 3.10: recorded as one statement with its four embedded features (inclusive practices, sustainable development, employability, information management), not one record per item.
- 3.6: under the heading "Generic skills" but items 1 to 3 are knowledge and understanding; domains set by content.
- Levels inferred: fheq:6 for 1.13, 1.14, 1.28.b1 to b3, 2.14 and 4.2 (graduates of the honours degree); each generic skill refines fheq:6 (FHEQ 4.15) and each master's item fheq:7 (FHEQ 4.17), checked against std:doc:qaa-fheq-2024-framework SL1 and std:ref:uk-framework R2.
- Themes: the issuer names five cross-cutting themes, including information management, for which no shared id existed: theme:information-management is new (for the shared theme list). Generative AI (3.16; 4.3.b36) takes theme:generative-ai. Employability sits with theme:enterprise, as the issuer joins them (EEE).
- Award: 2.22 to 2.25 and 3.21 take award "degree apprenticeship", a value outside the front matter's awardsCovered; for std.
- Codes inferred (SB-2): HECoS 100183 fire safety engineering (for Building Control and Fire Engineering), 100977 rural estate management (for Rural Land), 100150 construction and the built environment; CAH10-01-10 and CAH06-01-04 from them. No HECoS term names geospatial surveying; 100219 surveying is used, stated by the title.
- S7 relatedTo: the neighbouring benchmarks named by subject in 1.1 are linked to the editions held, inferred.
- SB5: the PSRBs' own requirements (RICS, CIOB, CICES, CABE), IfATE standards and EPA plans are not ingested.
- Back matter cites Annex D as QAA (2019) while 4.2 cites it inside the 2024 Frameworks; the 2024 document is used (std:doc:qaa-fheq-2024-framework). The back matter lists the Enterprise and Entrepreneurship guidance twice; it is not cited in the body.
- Issuer typos kept in sources: "handing electronic information" (1.28.b1), "intellection process" (3.4), "In England, , the award" (2.23).
- Paragraph spans from the skeleton carry a trailing sub-heading where the text runs on (1.13 "Sustainability", 2.14 "Partnership", 1.18 "Employability, enterprise and entrepreneurship (EEE)" and others); `source` keeps them as the span holds them.
- S9: not researched in P1.
- ST-12: versions here run to the lengths the content needs; no length range is yet set.
